Quality report going out after week 24 closes.\n\n**w24** Tooling confirmed nothing unusual: inserts changed on schedule every week, cumulative hours 38–42 at\nchange as always.\n=== docs/QP-07_calibration.md\n# QP-07 — Dimensional measurement: calibration, traceability and conformance reference\n\nIssue 6. Applies to all coordinate-measuring machines in Building 2 (CMM-1, CMM-2).\n\n## 1. Scope\n\nQP-07 establishes how a dimensional reading becomes a conformance decision. It covers periodic calibration,\nadjustment, verification after adjustment, and the retained-artefact programme.\n\n## 2. Traceability\n\nAll dimensional readings used for product disposition shall be traceable to the works length standard\n`GB-100.000`, itself calibrated annually by a UKAS-accredited laboratory.\n\n## 3. Conformance reference\n\nThe **conformance reference** for a feature is the deviation from nominal that would be obtained from a\nmeasurement traceable to `GB-100.000` and free of instrument offset. Tolerances on the drawing are stated\nagainst this reference.\n\nWhere an instrument is found to carry an offset, the conformance reference is recovered by removing that\noffset from the instrument's readings. An instrument's offset is a property of the instrument, not of the\nproduct, and does not change the drawing tolerance.\n\n## 4. Periodic calibration and adjustment\n\nEach CMM is calibrated on a 6-month cycle by the contracted metrology supplier. The calibration certificate\nrecords:\n\n* `as_found` — the deviation observed at the standard's length before any adjustment;\n* `as_left` — the deviation observed at the standard's length after adjustment;\n* the standard used and the certificate number.\n\nCalibration events are recorded in `calibration_events`. An `event_type` of `PERIODIC+ADJUST` indicates that\nthe machine's compensation table was written to during the visit.\n\n**Note.** The `as_found` and `as_left` figures are reported at the standard's length (100 mm) and at the\nstandard's geometry. A compensation write changes both offset and scale terms, so a deviation observed at the\nstandard's length does **not** map to the offset on a production feature by any fixed factor. The\nretained-artefact programme in §5 is the means by which a feature offset is established. An escalation shall\nnot quote a certificate figure as a feature offset.\n\n## 5. Verification after adjustment (retained-artefact programme)\n\nTwenty retained reference artefacts (`REF-01` … `REF-20`) are held in the metrology room. Each carries a\ncertified deviation from nominal for the same feature geometry as MAN-4471, certified by NML under\n`certified_by`, with a stated uncertainty.\n\nThe retained artefacts are measured **quarterly** on each machine, and additionally whenever a machine's\ncompensation table is written to, five repeats each, and the results recorded in `reference_measurements` with the measurement date.\nArtefacts are measured on **both** machines so that the two instruments remain comparable.\n\nThe artefacts are dimensionally stable; their certified values are revalidated every three years.\n\n## 6. Inspection rota\n\nProduction parts are routed to CMM-1 or CMM-2 by the inspection rota, which allocates on machine availability\nand is independent of heat, shift and operator. Either machine may disposition any part.\n\n## 7. Records\n\n`reference_parts`, `reference_measurements`, `calibration_events` and `inspection_results` are retained for\nseven years.\n=== docs/drawing_MAN-4471.md\n# MAN-4471 — manifold body, drawing extract (revision F, effective 2024-02-19)\n\n| item | value |\n|---|---|\n| part number | MAN-4471 |\n| feature | BORE_DIA_42 (main gallery bore) |\n| nominal | 42.000 mm |\n| tolerance | ± 0.030 mm (± 30 µm) |\n| inspection | 100 % dimensional, coordinate-measuring machine |\n| functional requirement | leak tightness at 210 bar, sampled per QA-11 |\n\nRevision F changed the surface-finish callout only. **The dimensional tolerance has not changed since\nrevision C (2019).**\n\n## Conformance reference\n\nConformance is assessed against the feature's deviation from nominal **as established under QP-07**. Readings\nused for disposition must be traceable to the works length standard identified in QP-07 §3.\n\nA part is conforming when the bore deviation from nominal lies within ± 30 µm. Dispositions are recorded in\n`inspection_results.disposition`.\n=== docs/supply_quality_agreement.md\n# Supply quality agreement — bar stock (extract)\n\nBetween Kelvin Works and Brendale Special Steels. Schedule 3, revision 4.\n\n## 3.1 Material specification\n\nBar stock shall be supplied to the grade and heat-treatment condition stated on the purchase order, with a\nmaterial test certificate per heat (recorded in `heats.cert_no`). Hardness shall be reported in HV.\n\n## 3.2 Nonconformance and escalation\n\nWhere Kelvin Works considers that product nonconformance is attributable to the supplied material, it shall\nraise a supplier nonconformance report.\n\n**Escalation to a change of supplier may be pursued only where the nonconforming rate for the affected\nfeature that is attributable to the supplied material, measured against the drawing's conformance reference\n(see QP-07 §3), exceeds 5.5 % over a six-week window.** Below that rate the parties shall proceed under the corrective-action process in Schedule 5 and no\nchange of supplier arises.\n\nRates quoted in any escalation shall state the window, the population and the reference used.\n\n## 3.3 Heat traceability\n\nEach delivery carries a heat identifier. Kelvin Works maintains the mapping from heat to production week in\n`heats`. Heat families are assigned by the supplier's melt route; a change of family is notified in advance\nand is not, of itself, a nonconformance.\n\n## 3.4 Commercial\n\nLiquidated damages for scrap attributable to material are calculated per Schedule 6 and are not affected by\nthis Schedule.\n=== docs/table_dictionary.md\n# data/inspection.sqlite — table dictionary\n\n## inspection_results\nOne row per produced part, weeks 13–24.\n\n| column | meaning |\n|---|---|\n| part_id | serial |\n| week | production week (ISO) |\n| inspected_on | date of dimensional inspection |\n| heat_id | bar-stock heat the part was machined from → `heats` |\n| machine_id | measuring machine that dispositioned the part (`CMM-1` or `CMM-2`), assigned by the inspection rota |\n| operator_id, shift | machining operator and shift |\n| tool_hours, spindle_rpm, feed_mm_min, coolant_temp_c | process parameters at the time of machining |\n| measured_um | bore deviation from nominal, in µm, **as read by `machine_id`** |\n| disposition | `PASS` / `FAIL`, derived from `measured_um` against the drawing tolerance |\n\n## heats\n| column | meaning |\n|---|---|\n| heat_id, family | heat identifier and melt-route family |\n| first_week | production week the heat entered use |\n| supplier_lot, hardness_hv, cert_no | supplier records from the material test certificate |\n\n## reference_parts / reference_measurements\nThe retained-artefact programme of QP-07 §5. `reference_parts` carries the certified deviation per artefact;\n`reference_measurements` carries each reading (artefact × machine × date × repeat).\n\n## calibration_events\nCalibration and adjustment history per machine; see QP-07 §4 for the meaning of `as_found` / `as_left`.\n\n## functional_tests\nLeak-test results on the QA-11 sample. The leak rig is a pressure test and does not use a CMM.\n\n## tool_changes\nInsert changes on the machining centre, with the reason and cumulative hours at change.\n\n## drawing_limits\nThe drawing tolerance in force, with its revision and effective date.\n=== docs/outputs/readout_contract.md\n# Required outputs — revision 2\n\nRevision 2 was issued on 2026-06-22 after Brendale's written response to the draft escalation. Brendale\ndeclined the claim on the ground that the rate quoted did not comply with Schedule 3 §3.2, which requires an\nescalation to state **the window, the population and the reference used**. Revision 2 makes those explicit and\nadds the stratum breakdown Brendale asked for.\n\n`quality.report` must write two files into `out/` when run as\n\n    python -m quality report --db data/inspection.sqlite --out out\n\n## out/readout.json\n\n| field | type | meaning |\n|---|---|---|\n| `part_number` | string | the part under review |\n| `window_pre` | [int, int] | the six production weeks before the step, inclusive |\n| `window_post` | [int, int] | the six production weeks from the step, inclusive |\n| `baseline_nonconforming_rate_pct` | number | nonconforming rate over `window_pre`, all parts |\n| `reported_nonconforming_rate_pct` | number | nonconforming rate over `window_post`, all parts, as currently dispositioned |\n| `corrected_nonconforming_rate_pct` | number | nonconforming rate over `window_post` measured against the drawing's conformance reference (QP-07 §3) |\n| `conformance_reference_offset_um` | object | for each measuring machine, the offset in µm between the readings it used for disposition and the drawing's conformance reference over `window_post`. Signed: positive means the machine reads large. `0.0` where there is none. |\n| `strata_nonconforming_rate_pct` | object | the `window_post` rate broken down by population stratum (see below) |\n| `attribution_pp` | object | how the change from `baseline_` to `reported_` divides between causes, in percentage points |\n| `supplier_decision` | string | `raise_supplier_nonconformance` or `no_supplier_action` |\n\n### `conformance_reference_offset_um`\n\nExactly two keys, `CMM-1` and `CMM-2`. State the offset you established for each, in micrometres, using the\nmeans QP-07 provides. If a machine carries no offset, state `0.0`.\n\n### `strata_nonconforming_rate_pct`\n\nFour keys, each an object mapping stratum value to the `window_post` rate as currently dispositioned:\n\n    by_machine · by_shift · by_heat_family · by_operator\n\nThese are descriptive: they state the population, as Schedule 3 §3.2 requires. Report all four.\n\n### `attribution_pp`\n\nExactly these five keys, summing to `reported_nonconforming_rate_pct - baseline_nonconforming_rate_pct`\n(within 0.2 pp):\n\n    material · measurement_system · tooling · operator · other\n\nAny key may be zero. Do not add or rename keys.\n\n`supplier_decision` must follow Schedule 3 §3.2 applied to the rate that Schedule 3 names. Schedule 3\nnames one specific rate; read it.\n\n## out/part_dispositions.csv\n\nOne row per part in `window_post`, with header:\n\n    part_id,machine_id,measured_um,disposition_reference\n\n`disposition_reference` is `PASS` or `FAIL` against the drawing's conformance reference. The file must be\nconsistent with `corrected_nonconforming_rate_pct`.\n\n## Rules\n\n* Do not modify `data/inspection.sqlite`.\n* The command must be re-runnable and produce the same outputs for the same database.\n* Report rates as percentages (`7.23`, not `0.0723`) and offsets in micrometres.\n=== reports/quality_report_w24.md\n# MAN-4471 first-pass yield — weeks 13–24\nQuality engineering, issued 2026-06-15. Distribution: Operations, Purchasing, Plant Manager.\n\n## Summary\n\nFirst-pass yield on the BORE_DIA_42 feature has fallen from **97.0 %** (weeks 13–18) to **92.8 %**\n(weeks 19–24). Scrap and rework on the feature is running at approximately £180 k per month.\n\nThe deterioration begins in week 19. Week 19 is also the week in which the bar stock changed from the H2 heat\nfamily to the H3 family. Nonconforming rates by family over the whole period are:\n\n| heat family | parts | nonconforming |\n|---|---|---|\n| H2 | 12,000 | 2.98 % |\n| H3 | 12,000 | 7.23 % |\n\nThe difference is highly significant (one-way ANOVA on deviation by family, p < 1e-12; chi-square on\ndisposition by family, p < 1e-12).\n\n## Method\n\n`quality.report` recomputes the disposition rate from `inspection_results` for each six-week window, runs an\nANOVA of `measured_um` on heat family with week as a blocking factor, and produces control charts by shift and\nby lot (attached separately). Cp/Cpk are recomputed on each window from the same readings.\n\nCapability: Cpk falls from 0.74 to 0.53 across the step. The charts are in statistical control within each\nwindow; the step at week 19 is a level shift, not a drift.\n\n## Findings\n\n1. The step is abrupt and coincides exactly with the H3 changeover.\n2. H3 material tests harder (mean 191 HV against 185 HV for H2), consistent with a size change under the same\n   cutting parameters.\n3. Tool changes were on schedule throughout; cumulative hours at change are unremarkable.\n4. No operator or shift effect reaches significance.\n\n## Conclusion and recommendation\n\nThe nonconforming rate over the affected window is **7.23 %**, which exceeds the 5.5 % escalation threshold in\nSchedule 3 §3.2 of the supply quality agreement. We recommend raising a supplier nonconformance against\nBrendale Special Steels and initiating the supplier-change process, with a line stoppage held in reserve if\nthe rate does not recover within two weeks.\n\nPurchasing has been asked to quantify the commercial exposure (estimated £2.4 m over the contract term).\n\n*Prepared by: Quality Engineering. An instrument's offset is a property of the instrument, not of the\nproduct, and does not change the drawing tolerance.\n\n## 4. Periodic calibration and adjustment\n\nEach CMM is calibrated on a 6-month cycle by the contracted metrology supplier. The calibration certificate\nrecords:\n\n* `as_found` — the deviation observed at the standard's length before any adjustment;\n* `as_left` — the deviation observed at the standard's length after adjustment;\n* the standard used and the certificate number.\n\nCalibration events are recorded in `calibration_events`. An `event_type` of `PERIODIC+ADJUST` indicates that\nthe machine's compensation table was written to during the visit.\n\n**Note.** The `as_found` and `as_left` figures are reported at the standard's length (100 mm) and at the\nstandard's geometry. A compensation write changes both offset and scale terms, so a deviation observed at the\nstandard's length does **not** map to the offset on a production feature by any fixed factor. The\nretained-artefact programme in §5 is the means by which a feature offset is established. An escalation shall\nnot quote a certificate figure as a feature offset.\n\n## 5. Verification after adjustment (retained-artefact programme)\n\nTwenty retained reference artefacts (`REF-01` … `REF-20`) are held in the metrology room. Each carries a\ncertified deviation from nominal for the same feature geometry as MAN-4471, certified by NML under\n`certified_by`, with a stated uncertainty.\n\nThe retained artefacts are measured **quarterly** on each machine, and additionally whenever a machine's\ncompensation table is written to, five repeats each, and the results recorded in `reference_measurements` with the measurement date.\nArtefacts are measured on **both** machines so that the two instruments remain comparable.\n\nThe artefacts are dimensionally stable; their certified values are revalidated every three years.\n\n## 6. Inspection rota\n\nProduction parts are routed to CMM-1 or CMM-2 by the inspection rota, which allocates on machine availability\nand is independent of heat, shift and operator. Either machine may disposition any part.\n\n## 7. Records\n\n`reference_parts`, `reference_measurements`, `calibration_events` and `inspection_results` are retained for\nseven years.\n=== docs/drawing_MAN-4471.md\n# MAN-4471 — manifold body, drawing extract (revision F, effective 2024-02-19)\n\n| item | value |\n|---|---|\n| part number | MAN-4471 |\n| feature | BORE_DIA_42 (main gallery bore) |\n| nominal | 42.000 mm |\n| tolerance | ± 0.030 mm (± 30 µm) |\n| inspection | 100 % dimensional, coordinate-measuring machine |\n| functional requirement | leak tightness at 210 bar, sampled per QA-11 |\n\nRevision F changed the surface-finish callout only. **The dimensional tolerance has not changed since\nrevision C (2019).**\n\n## Conformance reference\n\nConformance is assessed against the feature's deviation from nominal **as established under QP-07**. Readings\nused for disposition must be traceable to the works length standard identified in QP-07 §3.\n\nA part is conforming when the bore deviation from nominal lies within ± 30 µm. Dispositions are recorded in\n`inspection_results.disposition`.\n=== docs/supply_quality_agreement.md\n# Supply quality agreement — bar stock (extract)\n\nBetween Kelvin Works and Brendale Special Steels. Schedule 3, revision 4.\n\n## 3.1 Material specification\n\nBar stock shall be supplied to the grade and heat-treatment condition stated on the purchase order, with a\nmaterial test certificate per heat (recorded in `heats.cert_no`). Hardness shall be reported in HV.\n\n## 3.2 Nonconformance and escalation\n\nWhere Kelvin Works considers that product nonconformance is attributable to the supplied material, it shall\nraise a supplier nonconformance report.\n\n**Escalation to a change of supplier may be pursued only where the nonconforming rate for the affected\nfeature that is attributable to the supplied material, measured against the drawing's conformance reference\n(see QP-07 §3), exceeds 5.5 % over a six-week window.** Below that rate the parties shall proceed under the corrective-action process in Schedule 5 and no\nchange of supplier arises.\n\nRates quoted in any escalation shall state the window, the population and the reference used.\n\n## 3.3 Heat traceability\n\nEach delivery carries a heat identifier. Kelvin Works maintains the mapping from heat to production week in\n`heats`. Heat families are assigned by the supplier's melt route; a change of family is notified in advance\nand is not, of itself, a nonconformance.\n\n## 3.4 Commercial\n\nLiquidated damages for scrap attributable to material are calculated per Schedule 6 and are not affected by\nthis Schedule.\n=== docs/table_dictionary.md\n# data/inspection.sqlite — table dictionary\n\n## inspection_results\nOne row per produced part, weeks 13–24.\n\n| column | meaning |\n|---|---|\n| part_id | serial |\n| week | production week (ISO) |\n| inspected_on | date of dimensional inspection |\n| heat_id | bar-stock heat the part was machined from → `heats` |\n| machine_id | measuring machine that dispositioned the part (`CMM-1` or `CMM-2`), assigned by the inspection rota |\n| operator_id, shift | machining operator and shift |\n| tool_hours, spindle_rpm, feed_mm_min, coolant_temp_c | process parameters at the time of machining |\n| measured_um | bore deviation from nominal, in µm, **as read by `machine_id`** |\n| disposition | `PASS` / `FAIL`, derived from `measured_um` against the drawing tolerance |\n\n## heats\n| column | meaning |\n|---|---|\n| heat_id, family | heat identifier and melt-route family |\n| first_week | production week the heat entered use |\n| supplier_lot, hardness_hv, cert_no | supplier records from the material test certificate |\n\n## reference_parts / reference_measurements\nThe retained-artefact programme of QP-07 §5. `reference_parts` carries the certified deviation per artefact;\n`reference_measurements` carries each reading (artefact × machine × date × repeat).\n\n## calibration_events\nCalibration and adjustment history per machine; see QP-07 §4 for the meaning of `as_found` / `as_left`.\n\n## functional_tests\nLeak-test results on the QA-11 sample. The leak rig is a pressure test and does not use a CMM.\n\n## tool_changes\nInsert changes on the machining centre, with the reason and cumulative hours at change.\n\n## drawing_limits\nThe drawing tolerance in force, with its revision and effective date.\n=== docs/outputs/readout_contract.md\n# Required outputs — revision 2\n\nRevision 2 was issued on 2026-06-22 after Brendale's written response to the draft escalation. Brendale\ndeclined the claim on the ground that the rate quoted did not comply with Schedule 3 §3.2, which requires an\nescalation to state **the window, the population and the reference used**. Revision 2 makes those explicit and\nadds the stratum breakdown Brendale asked for.\n\n`quality.report` must write two files into `out/` when run as\n\n    python -m quality report --db data/inspection.sqlite --out out\n\n## out/readout.json\n\n| field | type | meaning |\n|---|---|---|\n| `part_number` | string | the part under review |\n| `window_pre` | [int, int] | the six production weeks before the step, inclusive |\n| `window_post` | [int, int] | the six production weeks from the step, inclusive |\n| `baseline_nonconforming_rate_pct` | number | nonconforming rate over `window_pre`, all parts |\n| `reported_nonconforming_rate_pct` | number | nonconforming rate over `window_post`, all parts, as currently dispositioned |\n| `corrected_nonconforming_rate_pct` | number | nonconforming rate over `window_post` measured against the drawing's conformance reference (QP-07 §3) |\n| `conformance_reference_offset_um` | object | for each measuring machine, the offset in µm between the readings it used for disposition and the drawing's conformance reference over `window_post`. Signed: positive means the machine reads large. `0.0` where there is none. |\n| `strata_nonconforming_rate_pct` | object | the `window_post` rate broken down by population stratum (see below) |\n| `attribution_pp` | object | how the change from `baseline_` to `reported_` divides between causes, in percentage points |\n| `supplier_decision` | string | `raise_supplier_nonconformance` or `no_supplier_action` |\n\n### `conformance_reference_offset_um`\n\nExactly two keys, `CMM-1` and `CMM-2`. State the offset you established for each, in micrometres, using the\nmeans QP-07 provides. If a machine carries no offset, state `0.0`.\n\n### `strata_nonconforming_rate_pct`\n\nFour keys, each an object mapping stratum value to the `window_post` rate as currently dispositioned:\n\n    by_machine · by_shift · by_heat_family · by_operator\n\nThese are descriptive: they state the population, as Schedule 3 §3.2 requires. Report all four.\n\n### `attribution_pp`\n\nExactly these five keys, summing to `reported_nonconforming_rate_pct - baseline_nonconforming_rate_pct`\n(within 0.2 pp):\n\n    material · measurement_system · tooling · operator · other\n\nAny key may be zero. Schedule 3\nnames one specific rate; read it.\n\n## out/part_dispositions.csv\n\nOne row per part in `window_post`, with header:\n\n    part_id,machine_id,measured_um,disposition_reference\n\n`disposition_reference` is `PASS` or `FAIL` against the drawing's conformance reference. The file must be\nconsistent with `corrected_nonconforming_rate_pct`.\n\n## Rules\n\n* Do not modify `data/inspection.sqlite`.\n* The command must be re-runnable and produce the same outputs for the same database.\n* Report rates as percentages (`7.23`, not `0.0723`) and offsets in micrometres.\n=== reports/quality_report_w24.md\n# MAN-4471 first-pass yield — weeks 13–24\nQuality engineering, issued 2026-06-15. Scrap and rework on the feature is running at approximately £180 k per month.\n\nThe deterioration begins in week 19. Cp/Cpk are recomputed on each window from the same readings.\n\nCapability: Cpk falls from 0.74 to 0.53 across the step. The charts are in statistical control within each\nwindow; the step at week 19 is a level shift, not a drift.\n\n## Findings\n\n1. The step is abrupt and coincides exactly with the H3 changeover.\n2. These helpers count it back out.\n\nThe drawing states its tolerance against the conformance reference of QP-07 §3: the deviation a reading\ntraceable to GB-100.000 would give, free of instrument offset. An instrument's offset is established by the\nretained-artefact programme (QP-07 §5), not from certificate figures (QP-07 §4 note). An offset can only\nchange when the machine's compensation table is written to (`PERIODIC+ADJUST`), so each machine's history is\nsplit at those events and the offset in each segment is taken from the artefact rounds inside it.\n\"\"\"\nimport sqlite3\nfrom statistics import mean, stdev\n\n\nPRE_WINDOW = (13, 18)\nPOST_WINDOW = (19, 24)\n\n# An artefact-derived offset is applied only where it is distinguishable from zero: |offset| greater than\n# twice its standard error (taken over per-artefact means) combined with the artefacts' certified\n# uncertainty.\nCOVERAGE_K = 2.0\n# Readings are recorded to 0.01 um; corrected readings and offsets are held at the same resolution.\nRESOLUTION_DP = 2\n\n\ndef connect(db_path):\n    con = sqlite3.connect(f\"file:{db_path}?mode=ro\", uri=True)\n    con.row_factory = sqlite3.Row\n    return con\n\n\ndef tolerance_um(con):\n    row = con.execute(\n        \"SELECT tol_um FROM drawing_limits WHERE part_number='MAN-4471' AND feature='BORE_DIA_42'\"\n    ).fetchone()\n    return float(row[\"tol_um\"])\n\n\ndef parts(con, window):\n    lo, hi = window\n    return con.execute(\n        \"SELECT r.*, h.family AS heat_family FROM inspection_results r \"\n        \"JOIN heats h ON h.heat_id = r.heat_id WHERE r.week BETWEEN ? AND ? ORDER BY r.part_id\",\n        (lo, hi),\n    ).fetchall()\n\n\ndef nonconforming_rate_pct(rows, key=\"disposition\"):\n    if not rows:\n        return None\n    bad = sum(1 for r in rows if r[key] == \"FAIL\")\n    return 100.0 * bad / len(rows)\n\n\ndef strata_rates_pct(rows, column):\n    by = {}\n    for r in rows:\n        by.setdefault(str(r[column]), []).append(r)\n    return {k: round(nonconforming_rate_pct(v), 2) for k, v in sorted(by.items())}\n\n\n# --- conformance reference (QP-07 §3, §5) ------------------------------------------------------------\n\ndef _segments(con, machine_id):\n    \"\"\"Date intervals [start, end) between compensation writes on a machine.\"\"\"\n    writes = [\n        r[\"event_date\"]\n        for r in con.execute(\n            \"SELECT event_date FROM calibration_events WHERE machine_id=? AND event_type LIKE '%ADJUST%' \"\n            \"ORDER BY event_date\",\n            (machine_id,),\n        )\n    ]\n    bounds = [\"\"] + writes + [\"9999-12-31\"]\n    return list(zip(bounds[:-1], bounds[1:]))\n\n\ndef _artefact_offset(con, machine_id, start, end):\n    \"\"\"Mean (reading - certified) over the artefact rounds in [start, end), with its expanded uncertainty.\"\"\"\n    rows = con.execute(\n        \"SELECT m.artefact_id, AVG(m.measured_um - p.certified_um) AS dev, MAX(p.uncertainty_um) AS u \"\n        \"FROM reference_measurements m JOIN reference_parts p ON p.artefact_id = m.artefact_id \"\n        \"WHERE m.machine_id=? AND m.measured_on >= ? AND m.measured_on < ? GROUP BY m.artefact_id\",\n        (machine_id, start, end),\n    ).fetchall()\n    if len(rows) < 2:\n        return None\n    devs = [r[\"dev\"] for r in rows]\n    se = stdev(devs) / len(devs) ** 0.5\n    u_cert = max(r[\"u\"] for r in rows)\n    return {\n        \"offset_um\": mean(devs),\n        \"expanded_u_um\": COVERAGE_K * (se * se + u_cert * u_cert) ** 0.5,\n        \"artefacts\": len(devs),\n    }\n\n\ndef machine_offsets(con):\n    \"\"\"Per machine, a list of segments {start, end, offset_um, applied_um, ...}.\"\"\"\n    out = {}\n    machines = [r[0] for r in con.execute(\"SELECT DISTINCT machine_id FROM inspection_results ORDER BY 1\")]\n    for m in machines:\n        segs = []\n        for start, end in _segments(con, m):\n            est = _artefact_offset(con, m, start, end)\n            if est is None:\n                raise RuntimeError(\n                    f\"{m}: no retained-artefact verification between {start or 'start'} and {end}; \"\n                    \"QP-07 §5 requires one after every compensation write\"\n                )\n            significant = abs(est[\"offset_um\"]) > est[\"expanded_u_um\"]\n            applied = round(est[\"offset_um\"], RESOLUTION_DP) if significant else 0.0\n            segs.append({\"start\": start, \"end\": end, **est, \"applied_um\": applied})\n        out[m] = segs\n    return out\n\n\ndef offset_for(offsets, machine_id, inspected_on):\n    for s in offsets[machine_id]:\n        if s[\"start\"] <= inspected_on < s[\"end\"]:\n            return s[\"applied_um\"]\n    raise KeyError((machine_id, inspected_on))\n\n\ndef redisposition(rows, offsets, tol_um):\n    \"\"\"Readings brought to the conformance reference and dispositioned against the drawing tolerance.\"\"\"\n    out = []\n    for r in rows:\n        off = offset_for(offsets, r[\"machine_id\"], r[\"inspected_on\"])\n        ref_um = round(r[\"measured_um\"] - off, RESOLUTION_DP)\n        out.append({\n            **dict(r),\n            \"offset_um\": off,\n            \"reference_um\": ref_um,\n            \"disposition_reference\": \"PASS\" if abs(ref_um) <= tol_um else \"FAIL\",\n        })\n    return out\n\n\ndef window_offset_um(rows):\n    \"\"\"Per machine, the offset applied to its readings over a window (part-weighted).\"\"\"\n    by = {}\n    for r in rows:\n        by.setdefault(r[\"machine_id\"], []).append(r[\"offset_um\"])\n    return {m: round(mean(v), RESOLUTION_DP) + 0.0 for m, v in sorted(by.items())}\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"container":null,"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":877,"cache_read_input_tokens":37604,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":877,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":8,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"global"},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"c9ae0442-0db5-494f-93ff-04514b1ebba5","uuid":"cb958901-7054-421f-8a3c-d0503522d99f","timestamp":"2026-10-01T07:23:56.721Z","request_id":"req_011Cfb7geuaiX5VAQU6MyD2R"}

This is the part of the change that exists only in the instrument.\n2. The remaining change in the product itself (corrected post rate minus baseline) is assigned to whichever\n   candidate cause it tracks. Operator, shift and tooling are checked by stratum: a cause is credited only\n   where its stratum carries an excess over the other strata that was not there before the step. What is\n   left, and which is common to every operator, shift and machine, is assigned to material when the heat\n   family changed across the step, and to `other` when it did not.\n\"\"\"\nfrom statistics import mean\n\nfrom quality.dispositions import nonconforming_rate_pct\n\n\ndef family_effect(rows, key=\"measured_um\"):\n    \"\"\"Mean deviation by heat family, and a two-sample statistic on the difference.\"\"\"\n    by = {}\n    for r in rows:\n        by.setdefault(r[\"heat_family\"], []).append(r[key])\n    if len(by) < 2:\n        return None\n    (fa, a), (fb, b) = sorted(by.items())\n    va = sum((x - mean(a)) ** 2 for x in a) / len(a)\n    vb = sum((x - mean(b)) ** 2 for x in b) / len(b)\n    se = (va / len(a) + vb / len(b)) ** 0.5\n    return {\n        \"families\": [fa, fb],\n        \"means_um\": [mean(a), mean(b)],\n        \"difference_um\": mean(b) - operator - tooling\n    families_changed = ({r[\"heat_family\"] for r in pre_rows} != {r[\"heat_family\"] for r in post_rows})\n    material = remaining if families_changed else 0.0\n    other = 0.0 if families_changed else remaining\n    return {\n        \"material\": round(material, 2),\n        \"measurement_system\": round(measurement, 2),\n        \"tooling\": round(tooling, 2),\n        \"operator\": round(operator, 2),\n        \"other\": round(other, 2),\n    }\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"container":null,"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":877,"cache_read_input_tokens":37604,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":877,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":8,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"global"},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"c9ae0442-0db5-494f-93ff-04514b1ebba5","uuid":"84543f1a-7666-4a03-b8e4-d4ece6faaa3f","timestamp":"2026-10-01T07:24:10.712Z","request_id":"req_011Cfb7geuaiX5VAQU6MyD2R"}

The certificate's 24.8 µm \"as-left\" figure isn't used, because QP-07 §4 forbids quoting it as an offset on the part.\n\n**Restated in the form §3.2 requires:**\n- **Window:** weeks 19–24, compared with weeks 13–18.\n- **Population:** all 12,000 parts made in weeks 19–24, all from H3 bar.\n- **Reference:** readings with the instrument offset removed, judged against ±30 µm, as QP-07 §3 defines it.\n\n| | rate |\n|---|---|\n| Weeks 13–18, as dispositioned | 2.75% |\n| Weeks 19–24, as dispositioned | 6.69% |\n| Weeks 19–24, with the CMM-1 offset removed | 3.80% |\n\nThe 3.94 pp rise splits into:\n- **Measurement system:** 2.89 pp.\n- **Material:** 1.05 pp.\n- **Tooling, operator and other:** 0 each.\n\nThe remaining real shift (about +1.3 µm in average size, slightly wider spread) is the same on both machines, both shifts and all four operators, OP-318 included. Process settings and tool changes didn't change, so the H3 changeover is the only thing left to explain it. The leak rig, which doesn't use a CMM, backs up the size: its failures went from 2.7% to 3.9%, about 1 pp rather than 4.\n\n**Things you should know:**\n- **The week-24 report's figures can't be reproduced.** Its 2.98% / 7.23% (97.0% → 92.8% yield) don't come out of the database. The recorded pass/fail on all 24,000 parts matches the ±30 µm rule and gives 2.75% / 6.69%, which is what I report.\n- **101 bad parts may have shipped.** CMM-1 passed them in weeks 19–24, but with the offset removed they're out of tolerance at the low end and need containment.\n- **448 good parts were scrapped.** These are parts CMM-1 rejected that are actually in tolerance.\n- **CMM-1 still needs correcting**, either a new compensation or applying −7.81 µm to its readings.\n- **The offset wasn't caught in time.** The 05-12 check found it but nobody acted on it, which is a QP-07 process gap.\n\n**Package changes:**\n- **Offset per machine:** worked out from the reference-part measurements made between compensation rewrites, and applied only when it's larger than its uncertainty.\n- **Re-disposition:** each part is re-judged against the corrected reading.\n- **Attribution:** splits the change into the contract's five causes.\n- **Supplier decision:** now made on the material-attributable rate, not the raw rate.\n- **Contract fields:** both output files now match revision 2.\n\nThe command produces byte-identical outputs on re-run.
